              "snippet": "\n### Increased Adoption of Cloud Outsourcing\n\nCloud infrastructure has revolutionized how businesses access data and applications, facilitating remote work and fostering global collaboration. Initially hailed for its cost-saving advantages and competitive edge, businesses now view it as an essential driver for innovation, agility, and success. \n\nBy 2024, the adoption of 【50†multi-cloud solutions†www.forbes.com】 is expected to rise significantly from 76% to 85%. As more businesses increasingly embrace cloud-based solutions, the cloud’s potential to reshape data governance amplifies.\n\n### Global Workforce Collaboration\n\nSorry, your browser doesn't support embedded videos.\n\nOutsourcing is a means to make the workforce more interconnected. From a way to navigate the talent crunch, cross-border recruitment has evolved to become the ideal strategy for accessing a global staffing pool. Rather than limiting recruitment efforts to the best candidates in your area, this strategy enables businesses to scout and recruit the most qualified professionals worldwide.\n\nOutsourcing also provides a competitive edge by connecting businesses with a global workforce. Employees engaging in a diverse outsourcing environment gain invaluable exposure to various cultural perspectives and work styles. \n\nThis exposure not only enhances their understanding of diversity but also cultivates skills in communication, empathy, and collaboration across different cultures. As a result, employees become better equipped to build authentic relationships with international clients, fostering deeper connections and understanding, which are vital for successful global partnerships.\n\n### Focus on Sustainability Outsourcing \n\nIt has become an ethical imperative for companies to adopt 【51†corporate social responsibility】 (CSR) objectives that empower communities, build trust with stakeholders, and nurture a sustainable future for their business and society. Besides helping companies stay socially accountable, CSR enhances goodwill and protects against customers’ hostile perceptions towards the brand.\n\nAs environmental and social responsibility continue to gain prominence, consider outsourcing partners that align with your organization’s sustainability goals. Evaluate their ecological footprint, commitment to ethical labor practices, and social responsibility initiatives. This proactive approach cultivates a responsible supply chain and generates positive stakeholder resonance.\n\n### Outsource Data Analytics for Informed Decision-Making\n\nBusinesses can no longer deny data’s importance in the digital age. A recent poll showed that many employees (41%) and employers (38%) 【52†value data analytics†www.shrm.org】 as knowledge critical in improving performance. Another study predicted that with a CAGR of 11.33%, the global data analytics outsourcing industry 【53†will hit†www.rtinsights.com】 $9.15 billion by 2030 — almost a threefold jump from only $3.87 billion in 2022.\n\nThese numbers reflect the significance of data in 2024. Data is a gold mine of information. Yet, without the expertise to extract insights, data exists as mere scattered particles within virtual space — which explains the growing trend of companies 【33†outsourcing data analytics】. This strategic move allows them to extract meaningful insights, facilitate informed decision-making, enhance customer experiences, and optimize operational processes.\n\n# Mastering Outsourcing: Best Practices for Success\n\nIn today’s dynamic market, outsourcing has become a cornerstone strategy for many organizations seeking efficiency, flexibility, and specialized expertise. \n\nLet us explore the best practices and strategies to leverage outsourcing better and pave the way for unparalleled success in your ventures.\n\n[Image 4: A mini infographic shows icons illustrating outsourcing best practices for 2024. ][Image 5: A mini infographic shows icons illustrating outsourcing best practices for 2024. ]\n\n### Formulate Clear Goals and Requirements\n\nBefore outsourcing, you must clearly understand your organization’s needs and goals. Assess your business processes and determine the pain points that stand in the way of success. \n\nOnce you’ve identified your weaknesses, it’s time to determine the specific outsourcing needs to address these internal challenges, whether they involve gaps in your workforce, qualifications, or essential business functions. This is a crucial reference point when communicating your requirements to your chosen outsourcing provider.\n\n### Choose the Optimal Outsourcing Destination \n\nSelecting the right outsourcing destination goes beyond cost considerations. You must consider time zone compatibility, cultural alignment, language proficiency, political stability, and legal frameworks. It would be best to balance cost-effectiveness and a conducive business atmosphere. \n\nFor offshoring, consider the Philippines, a top outsourcing destination in Asia that currently reigns as the “Call Center Capital of the World. Meanwhile, if you’re looking for a nearshore destination, Colombia, an emerging tech hub dubbed “Silicon Valley of Latin America,” is an equally strategic choice.\n\n### Establish Honest and Open Communication \n\nTrust is a powerful tool that can make or break your revenue streams. Research shows that 91% of business leaders find that 【54†maintaining trust†www.pwc.com】 can enhance their companies’ bottom line. When you and your outsourcing partner have established trust, they will be more inclined to work diligently for your projects.\n\nThe key to achieving trust is communication. In scenarios where certain misunderstandings are inevitable, businesses must always maintain open communication lines so both parties can resolve their differences respectfully. This builds a safe and secure environment for you and your outsourcing partner, paving the way for seamless collaboration. \n\n### Invest in Training and Development\n\nPrioritizing employee learning opportunities is a critical business focus for sustainable, enduring growth. But the reality is that the majority (71%) of employers experience 【55†upskilling difficulties†www.devry.edu】, with lack of budget and workforce (43%), worker motivation (41%), and prioritization strategies (32%) posing as primary obstacles. \n\nDespite these difficulties, businesses must find strategic and cost-effective means to upskill and reskill employees to stay agile amid economic uncertainty and global disruptions. When done right, businesses that upskill can yield higher returns on investments (ROIs) and profit margins than companies without training programs. \n\n### Monitor Performance and Quality\n\nSelecting the ideal outsourcing partner that fits your business requirements can be challenging. However, by defining the right key performance indicators (KPIs)",

              "snippet": "\nAs organizations grapple with the challenges of staying competitive in an era of rapid technological advancement and global interconnectedness, the role of outsourcing becomes increasingly pivotal. The trends that will shape outsourcing in 2024 not only address current business needs but also position organizations to be agile and resilient in the face of future uncertainties. Let’s dig into these seven compelling trends that are poised to rule the outsourcing realm in the coming year and beyond.\n\n### 1. Rise of Robotic Process Automation (RPA)\n\nRobotic Process Automation (RPA) is poised to redefine the outsourcing landscape in 2024. Organizations are increasingly integrating automation into their processes, allowing software robots to handle routine and repetitive tasks. This not only improves efficiency but also reduces the risk of errors. RPA is particularly impactful in back-office functions, such as data entry, invoice processing, and customer support, freeing up human resources for more complex and strategic tasks.\n\n### 2. Focus on Cybersecurity Outsourcing \n\nWith the escalating threat landscape in the digital realm, cybersecurity outsourcing is becoming a top priority for organizations. As cyber-attacks become more sophisticated, businesses are recognizing the need for specialized expertise to safeguard their sensitive data and systems. Outsourcing cybersecurity services ensures access to the latest threat intelligence, advanced technologies, and a dedicated team of experts to fortify defenses against evolving cyber threats.\n\n### 3. Increased Adoption of Cloud Outsourcing \n\nCloud computing continues to be a game-changer, and in 2024, its influence on outsourcing is set to grow. Organizations are leveraging cloud outsourcing for various functions, from data storage and processing to software development and testing. The cloud provides scalability, flexibility, and accessibility, enabling businesses to enhance their agility and respond swiftly to changing market dynamics.\n\n### 4. Global Workforce Collaboration \n\nThe geographical boundaries of outsourcing are expanding, with a surge in global workforce collaboration. Organizations are tapping into talent pools worldwide, fostering collaboration among teams from different corners of the globe. This trend is facilitated by advanced communication tools, project management platforms, and the growing acceptance of remote work. By harnessing diverse skill sets from various locations, organizations can drive innovation and maintain a competitive edge.\n\n### 5. Focus on Sustainability Outsourcing \n\nSustainability is no longer just a buzzword; it’s a business imperative. In 2024, outsourcing with a focus on sustainability will gain prominence. Yet, without the expertise to extract insights, data exists as mere scattered particles within virtual space — which explains the growing trend of companies 【33†outsourcing data analytics】. This strategic move allows them to extract meaningful insights, facilitate informed decision-making, enhance customer experiences, and optimize operational processes.\n\n# Mastering Outsourcing: Best Practices for Success\n\nIn today’s dynamic market, outsourcing has become a cornerstone strategy for many organizations seeking efficiency, flexibility, and specialized expertise. \n\nLet us explore the best practices and strategies to leverage outsourcing better and pave the way for unparalleled success in your ventures.\n\n[Image 4: A mini infographic shows icons illustrating outsourcing best practices for 2024. ][Image 5: A mini infographic shows icons illustrating outsourcing best practices for 2024. ]\n\n### Formulate Clear Goals and Requirements\n\nBefore outsourcing, you must clearly understand your organization’s needs and goals. Assess your business processes and determine the pain points that stand in the way of success. \n\nOnce you’ve identified your weaknesses, it’s time to determine the specific outsourcing needs to address these internal challenges, whether they involve gaps in your workforce, qualifications, or essential business functions. This is a crucial reference point when communicating your requirements to your chosen outsourcing provider.\n\n### Choose the Optimal Outsourcing Destination \n\nSelecting the right outsourcing destination goes beyond cost considerations. You must consider time zone compatibility, cultural alignment, language proficiency, political stability, and legal frameworks. It would be best to balance cost-effectiveness and a conducive business atmosphere. \n\nFor offshoring, consider the Philippines, a top outsourcing destination in Asia that currently reigns as the “Call Center Capital of the World. Meanwhile, if you’re looking for a nearshore destination, Colombia, an emerging tech hub dubbed “Silicon Valley of Latin America,” is an equally strategic choice.\n\n### Establish Honest and Open Communication \n\nTrust is a powerful tool that can make or break your revenue streams. Research shows that 91% of business leaders find that 【54†maintaining trust†www.pwc.com】 can enhance their companies’ bottom line. When you and your outsourcing partner have established trust, they will be more inclined to work diligently for your projects.\n\nThe key to achieving trust is communication. In scenarios where certain misunderstandings are inevitable, businesses must always maintain open communication lines so both parties can resolve their differences respectfully. This builds a safe and secure environment for you and your outsourcing partner, paving the way for seamless collaboration. \n\n### Invest in Training and Development\n\nPrioritizing employee learning opportunities is a critical business focus for sustainable, enduring growth. But the reality is that the majority (71%) of employers experience 【55†upskilling difficulties†www.devry.edu】, with lack of budget and workforce (43%), worker motivation (41%), and prioritization strategies (32%) posing as primary obstacles. \n\nDespite these difficulties, businesses must find strategic and cost-effective means to upskill and reskill employees to stay agile amid economic uncertainty and global disruptions. When done right, businesses that upskill can yield higher returns on investments (ROIs) and profit margins than companies without training programs. \n\n### Monitor Performance and Quality\n\nSelecting the ideal outsourcing partner that fits your business requirements can be challenging. However, by defining the right key performance indicators (KPIs)",

              "snippet": "\nAs organizations grapple with the challenges of staying competitive in an era of rapid technological advancement and global interconnectedness, the role of outsourcing becomes increasingly pivotal. The trends that will shape outsourcing in 2024 not only address current business needs but also position organizations to be agile and resilient in the face of future uncertainties. Let’s dig into these seven compelling trends that are poised to rule the outsourcing realm in the coming year and beyond.\n\n### 1. Rise of Robotic Process Automation (RPA)\n\nRobotic Process Automation (RPA) is poised to redefine the outsourcing landscape in 2024. Organizations are increasingly integrating automation into their processes, allowing software robots to handle routine and repetitive tasks. This not only improves efficiency but also reduces the risk of errors. RPA is particularly impactful in back-office functions, such as data entry, invoice processing, and customer support, freeing up human resources for more complex and strategic tasks.\n\n### 2. Focus on Cybersecurity Outsourcing \n\nWith the escalating threat landscape in the digital realm, cybersecurity outsourcing is becoming a top priority for organizations. As cyber-attacks become more sophisticated, businesses are recognizing the need for specialized expertise to safeguard their sensitive data and systems. Outsourcing cybersecurity services ensures access to the latest threat intelligence, advanced technologies, and a dedicated team of experts to fortify defenses against evolving cyber threats.\n\n### 3. Increased Adoption of Cloud Outsourcing \n\nCloud computing continues to be a game-changer, and in 2024, its influence on outsourcing is set to grow. Organizations are leveraging cloud outsourcing for various functions, from data storage and processing to software development and testing. The cloud provides scalability, flexibility, and accessibility, enabling businesses to enhance their agility and respond swiftly to changing market dynamics.\n\n### 4. Global Workforce Collaboration \n\nThe geographical boundaries of outsourcing are expanding, with a surge in global workforce collaboration. Organizations are tapping into talent pools worldwide, fostering collaboration among teams from different corners of the globe. This trend is facilitated by advanced communication tools, project management platforms, and the growing acceptance of remote work. By harnessing diverse skill sets from various locations, organizations can drive innovation and maintain a competitive edge.\n\n### 5. Focus on Sustainability Outsourcing \n\nSustainability is no longer just a buzzword; it’s a business imperative. In 2024, outsourcing with a focus on sustainability will gain prominence. Organizations are seeking partners who align with their environmental, social, and governance (ESG) goals. This includes outsourcing providers committed to eco-friendly practices, ethical labor standards, and social responsibility. Sustainability outsourcing not only enhances corporate social responsibility but also mitigates risks associated with environmental and social issues.\n\n### 6. Data Analytics Outsourcing for Informed Decision-Making \n\nData is a goldmine of insights, and organizations are increasingly turning to outsourcing for advanced data analytics capabilities. In 2024, the focus will be on harnessing data to make informed business decisions. 【23†Business Process Outsourcing provider】 with expertise in data analytics can help organizations derive meaningful insights from vast datasets, enabling strategic decision-making, personalized customer experiences, and optimized operational processes.\n\n### 7. Evolving Talent Models with Skill-Based Outsourcing \n\nThe traditional outsourcing model is evolving into a more dynamic and skill-based approach. In 2024, organizations will prioritize outsourcing specific skills rather than entire processes. This allows businesses to access niche expertise for short-term projects or specific needs without committing to long-term contracts. Skill-based outsourcing enhances agility, cost-effectiveness, and the ability to adapt to rapidly changing business requirements.  \n\n## Embracing the Future of Outsourcing \n\nAs organizations navigate the challenges and opportunities presented by a dynamic business environment, outsourcing emerges as a strategic ally. The trends shaping outsourcing in 2024 reflect a commitment to efficiency, innovation, and sustainability. Whether leveraging automation, fortifying cybersecurity defenses, or tapping into global talent pools, organizations embracing these trends are poised to thrive in an ever-evolving marketplace. As businesses continue to reassess their strategies, outsourcing remains a flexible and powerful tool to stay competitive and agile in the years to come.  \n\n## In Conclusion \n\nOur exploration of the outsourcing trends is set to define 2024. It’s evident that businesses, irrespective of their size or industry, are standing at the precipice of a transformative era. These trends not only respond to the immediate needs of efficiency, innovation, and security but also reflect a strategic vision for the future of outsourcing.\n\nEmbracing these trends offers organizations the opportunity to stay ahead of the curve, navigating the complexities of the modern business landscape with agility and foresight. Whether it’s harnessing the power of robotic process automation, fortifying cybersecurity defenses, or tapping into the global pool of talent, the outsourcing strategies of tomorrow are designed to not just meet but exceed the expectations of a rapidly evolving market.\n\nIn this dynamic landscape, the choice of an outsourcing partner becomes a critical decision. For those seeking a partner that combines cutting-edge technology, a global footprint, and a commitment to excellence, 【0†JindalX】 emerges as a beacon. With a proven track record in the logistics and technology sectors, JindalX stands ready to empower businesses in their outsourcing endeavors. As we stand on the threshold of 2024, the call to businesses is clear: embrace these outsourcing trends, chart a course for success, and consider JindalX as your trusted partner in this transformative journey. Let’s build a future of collaboration, innovation, and sustained growth together. Connect with JindalX to unlock the full potential of outsourcing in the years to come.\n\n[Image 5] \n\n### Customer Service Outsourcing Guide for Startups\n\nUnlock Major Pitfalls to Avoid and a Solid Foolproof Personalized CX strategy\n\n### Latest Posts\n\n【24† 】 \n\n【25†Customer Experience】 【26†Others】\n\n####  【24†People Power! 北九州市周辺｜ニフティ温泉†onsen.nifty.com】\n河内温泉「あじさいの湯」は、八幡東区河内地域で湧出した温泉と周辺の水辺や緑を活かした、多世代の市民が楽しく憩える温泉施設です。. 森林浴と温泉浴でゆったり、ほっこり。. 帆柱自然公園の山々に抱かれた、水と緑の山里にある隠れ湯です。.\n# 【15†若槻・あじさいの湯 | NPO法人ポータルサイト - 内閣府†www.npo-homepage.go.jp】\n長野市所有の高齢者施設「若槻老人憩の家」の精神を発展的に継承し、入浴事業と併せ、高齢者の通所介護事業等を主とした多機能施設事業の展開を通して、高齢者や障害者を支援する活動を行い、地域の福祉事業所化を目指し、地域住民参加型による活力 ...\n# 【16†【公式】千葉県松戸市のデイサービス | ヒューマンライフケアあじさいの湯 | 日帰り通所介護†kaigo.human-lifecare.jp】\n千葉県松戸市のデイサービス・ヒューマンライフケアあじさいの湯です！. 北松戸駅から松戸市立病院へと向か坂の途中にあるマンションの一角にあるデイサービスです。. 広々としたスペースで麻雀や陶芸、囲碁、体操、創作など様々な活動を行っています ...\n# 【17†若槻・あじさいの湯紹介ページ | activo（アクティボ）†activo.jp】\n若槻・あじさいの湯の概要ならactivo！. 若槻・あじさいの湯の概要 (住所長野県長野市大字三輪1320番地 ダイアパレス長野大通り413号 電話番号・TEL )や代表者 (西澤 哲氏)、活動理念、活動内容、従業員数、ジャンル (こども・教育, 地域活性化・まちづくり ...\n# 【18†約4000株の綺麗なあじさい。もととりあじさい園 - WING†wing-r.com】\n直方市金剛山のふもとにある「もととりあじさい園」。毎年6月には、約4,000株のあじさいの美しい景色を見ることができます。27年前に地元の男性が、里山を美しくしようとあじさいを植えたのをきっかけに山林の整備が進められ、今では約4000株にまで増えたそうです。\n# 【19†【兵庫県】紫陽花(アジサイ)の名所・絶景スポット12選！見ごろや品種の情報も(2024)｜兵庫県はりまエリア(姫路・加古川など)の地域情報 ...†tanosu.com】\n6月〜7月は紫陽花(アジサイ)が見ごろを迎えるシーズン。土壌や品種によって青や紫、白などさまざまな色のアジサイが楽しめます。そこで、兵庫県内でたくさんのアジサイが眺められる寺や植物園、公園、珍しい品種のアジサイがあるスポットなど12カ所を厳選！開園期間や見ごろ、品種などの ...\nVisible: 0% -                 "type": "webpage",
                "title": "【あじさいの湯】足湯も楽しめる熊本県宇土市の公営温泉に行ってきた | 八代連絡帳",
                "url": "https://8246renraku.net/archives/ajisainoyu.html",
                "text": "\n熊本県宇土市にある公営温泉「あじさいの湯」に行ってきました。敷地内には無料の足湯もありますよ。また、近くにはアジサイの名所として有名な住吉自然公園もあります。営業時間や料金等確認されて行かれてください。\n",
                "pub_date": "2024-06-18T09:32:00.0000000Z",
                "extra": null

                "url": "https://city-uto.com/archives/251",
                "text": "\n地下900mから湧き出す100％天然のアルカリ性単純温泉。2019年、往来の温泉施設に加え、足湯や寝ころび湯などが登場！足湯は館外にあるので入館料無しでの利用も可。\n",

                "url": "https://www.rk-hotel.com/hotspring.html",

          "content_type": "text",
          "parts": [
            "「あじさいの湯」は、いくつかの場所に存在しますが、以下の2つの主な場所が知られています：\n\n1. **熊本県宇土市のあじさいの湯**:\n   - 住所：熊本県宇土市浦田町51\n   - 地下900mから湧き出す100%天然のアルカリ性単純温泉が特徴です。大浴場、サウナ、足湯などの施設が整っています【9†source】【10†source】。\n\n2. **北海道函館市のニヤマ温泉あじさいの湯**:\n   - 住所：北海道亀田郡七飯町字仁山670\n   - 北海道の美しい自然に囲まれた温泉施設で、リラックスできる環境が提供されています【9†source】【10†source】。\n\nこれらの施設は、それぞれ異なる地域にあり、特色も異なりますが、いずれも訪れる価値のある温泉地です。"
          ]
        },

            {
              "start_ix": 149,

              "metadata": {
                "type": "webpage",
                "title": "About Japanese School Uniforms: Symbols of Freedom, Rebellion, and Fashion | LIVE JAPAN travel guide",
                "url": "https://livejapan.com/en/in-tokyo/in-pref-tokyo/in-tokyo_suburbs/article-a0002110/",
                "text": "\nThere, Japanese women wore the latest fashion from Paris while indulging in Western ballroom dancing, French cuisine, and more. However, Western style was limited to the women of Japan's high society during that time.   \n\nFollowing that, a style that blended Japanese and Western aesthetics arose, pairing Western-style accessories with authentically Japanese kimono.   \n\nEventually, around 1920, girls' school uniforms made the full change from hakama to Western fashion. Especially sailor-inspired outfits enjoyed great popularity among female students.  \n\nThe modern sailor uniform gained popularity as it pushed other styles aside. During this time, the Japanese uniforms weren't ready-made garments, but people had to make them at their own expense. If you had the 【136†fabric】 and pattern at hand, making a sailor-style uniform was relatively easy – another reason for its popularity.\n\n[Image 5: Left: early sailor-style uniform / Right: early Western-style uniform]\n\nLeft: early sailor-style uniform / Right: early Western-style uniform\n\n##  Experimenting with Different Japanese Fashion Styles\n\nAccording to Tomoko 【69†Namba】, girls have tried to wear their uniforms in cute or cool ways since the very beginning.  \n\n\"The school uniform started as a symbol of the elite, so just by wearing one, you were sure to attract admiration and envious glances. Besides, the uniform regulations were not strict before the war, so it was possible to use nice 【136†fabric】 and to adjust it to your own body shape, or style it in a way you like.\"\n\n[Image 6: Even today, a lot of Japanese middle school uniforms have a high collar and are in the sailor style. However, there are also schools that switch to blazers, as many go abroad for school trips and the high collar looks too much like a military uniform.]\n\nEven today, a lot of Japanese middle school uniforms have a high collar and are in the sailor style. However, there are also schools that switch to blazers, as many go abroad for school trips and the high collar looks too much like a military uniform.\n\n\"From the 1920s, girls' uniforms sported a shorter top combined with a long skirt. If the students didn't like the standard style, they seemed to have changed it to a more fashionable version. At that time, pleated skirts were en vogue. Even at schools that had regulations for how many folds a skirt needs to have, records 【137†show】 that the students would wear the small pleated style on days without uniform inspection.\"\n\n##  Sukeban – The “Delinquent Girl” Changes the Japanese School Uniform\n\n[Image 7: A modified school uniform. There were extreme trends such as flamboyant linings of boys’ collars, very wide pants or particularly short sailor-style tops, very long skirts, and so on.]\n\nA modified school uniform. There were extreme trends such as flamboyant linings of boys’ collars, very wide pants or particularly short sailor-style tops, very long skirts, and so on.\n\nThe background of these changes was the sukeban boom. The term sukeban is composed of the Japanese words for \"female\" and \"juvenile gang leader.\" It means just that – girl gangster boss. The male version is called tsuppari or banchō.   \n\nThese gang bosses were known for their daring disregard of school regulations and rules, as well as general bad behavior, such as threatening other students, not paying attention in lessons, and even organizing groups to gain influence and power. Proper ruffians, so to say.  \n\nThe looks played an important part as well. Hair dye and perm were common for both genders, these \"delinquent girls\" also wore heavy makeup, and uniforms were changed entirely to create an intimidating look, like the one shown in the picture above.   \n\nThe student bag that went with an outfit like that was often wrangled to such a flat shape that it was practically impossible to put anything inside. It represented the sukeban's anti-stance towards the school and its strict rules perfectly.  \n\nFrom a regular student's viewpoint, the sukeban style was intimidating yet also cool and somewhat fascinating. It was a time in which kids were under a lot of social pressure, fiercely competitive entrance exams being only one example, so the rebellious and bold sukeban seemed to strike a chord with many students.   \n\nBoth sukeban and tsuppari became somewhat of a phenomenon, bringing forth movies, books, 【138†manga】, bands, and even a cat character who wore a sukeban-style uniform was created – and they all were massive hits.  \n\nThe behavior of these girls and boys got more extreme, and eventually, violence at school and juvenile delinquency became a problem for Japanese society. \"Depravity starts with the clothes,\" was a phrase used by the Metropolitan Police Department, alongside an explanation of the sukeban style and a guide on how to identify said depravity in someone's outfit.   \n\nIt was such a significant topic, even the American magazine Newsweek picked it up. To combat this trend, both the school uniform industry and the schools themselves established clothing standards and enforced much stricter rules. It's an era with many regulations more stringent than the decades before.\n\n##  The Birth of Designer Brand Uniforms\n\nA direct result of these strict rules was the movement to abolish Japanese school uniforms altogether, stressing personal freedom and individuality.   \n\nIt was also the time when uniforms started to put value on looking stylish and switched to the now-popular blazer style, which has been thought of as rather unfashionable until then.   \n\nIn the wake of a declining birth rate, schools used stylish uniforms to attract students more aggressively and the clothes became a symbol of a school’s charm.  \n\nThis fashion-oriented strategy hit the mark just perfectly – the uniform you see in the image below created a flood of applications for its school.   \n\nIt was created ",
                "pub_date": null,
                "extra": {
                  "cited_message_idx": 9,

            {
              "start_ix": 159,

              "citation_format_type": "tether_og",

                "type": "webpage",
                "title": "From Tradition to Today: Japanese School Uniforms",
                "url": "https://learnjapanese123.com/japanese-school-uniforms/",
                "text": "\nDepending on the discipline level of any particular school, students may often wear different seasonal and activity uniforms within the same classroom during the day. Certain schools also regulate student hairstyles, footwear, piercings, and book bags, but these particular rules have become slightly more relaxed in the modern times, and at some schools are only adhered to on special occasions such as school opening/closing ceremonies or school photo days.\n\nIt is normal for uniforms to be worn outside of school areas, and you will often see students out in groups in the evenings and on the weekends still dressed in their school uniforms. As for the younger kids, although not many public elementary schools in Japan require uniforms, many private schools and public schools run by the central government still do so.\n\n[Image 12][Image 13]\n\nSource: https://www.kuropixel.com/japanese-and-american-school-differences/\n\n## Girls’ Uniforms\n\nWhen you think of Japanese schoolgirls, you likely picture them in sailor uniforms. Seira fuku (セーラー服 ) is a common style of uniform worn by female middle school students, occasionally by high school students, and even rarely by elementary school students. It was first introduced as a school uniform in 1920 in Heian Jogakuin (平安女学院) and 1921 by the principal of Fukuoka Jo Gakuin University (福岡女学院), Elizabeth Lee. The sailor design was modeled after the uniform that was bring used by the British Royal Navy at the time, which Elizabeth Lee had seen firsthand when she had been an exchange student in the United Kingdom.\n\nThe uniform typically consists of a blouse with a sailor-style collar and a pleated skirt. As mentioned, the uniforms change slightly in the summer summer and winter, so depending on the season, the sleeve length and fabric are adjusted accordingly. A ribbon is tied in the front and laced through a loop attached to the blouse. Although seira fuku does all generally follow a similar design, the ribbon collar can vary, sometimes instead having neckties, bolo ties, neckerchiefs, and bows. The most common colors are navy blue, white, grey, and black.\n\nThe shoes, socks, and other accessories are sometimes included as part of the school’s regulated uniform. The socks are typically navy or white, and usually come up quite high. Shoes are generally brown or black penny loafers.\n\nToday, the sailor uniform is usually associated with junior high schools, since a majority of high schools have adapted the more Western-style plaid skirts or blazers.\n\n[Image 14][Image 15]\n\n[Image 16][Image 17]\n\n## Boys’ Uniforms\n\nJapanese boys at most public and private schools in Japan wear unique uniforms as well. By far, the most common uniform for boys is the gakuran (学ラン). It consists of a square-shouldered top with a stand-up collar, five golden buttons, and black straight-legged pants.  The color of the uniform is normally black, but some schools use navy blue.\n\nThe gakuran is derived from the Waffenrock, a military uniform that was introduced to Prussia in 1842. The term gakuran is a combination of gaku (学) meaning “study” or “student”, and ran (らん or 蘭) meaning the Netherlands or, historically in Japan, the West in general. Therefore, gakuran literally translates as “Western student”.\n\nThe uniform has a standing collar top that buttons down from top-to-bottom, and straight-legged pants that are worn with a dark-colored belt. As for the shoes, boys usually wear penny loafers or sneakers with this uniform. Some schools may require the students to wear collar-pins representing the school and/or class rank. Traditionally, the gakuran was also worn along with a matching (usually black) cap, but this custom is less common in modern times.\n\nThe buttons, typically gold-colored, are usually decorated with the school emblem. The second button is the one closest to the heart, and it’s common practice for a boy to give the second button from the top of a his uniform to a female he is in love with, as a sort of confession. This practice was apparently made popular by a scene in a novel by Taijun Takeda.\n\n[Image 18][Image 19]\n\n[Image 20][Image 21]\n\n## Modern Styles & Trends\n\nWhile these seira fuku and gakuran styles of uniforms are still quite common today, many schools these days have moved to more Western-pattern catholic school uniforms. These uniforms consist of a white shirt, a tie, a blazer with the school crest, and tailored trousers for the boys and a plaid skirt with high socks for the girls.\n\nThese days, various schools are known for their particular uniforms. For former students, school uniforms can have a nostalgic characteristic, and are often associated with relatively carefree youth. For current present-day students, uniforms are sometimes modified as a means of exhibiting individualism. Students may try to create their own trends by shortening the skirt, removing the ribbon, wearing small patches or badges under the collar, or even wearing subtle amounts of makeup. However, this isn’t just a current trend – there have always been young people who have sought to express a little originality by bending school uniform rules.\n\nJust as with mainstream fashion, student school uniforms experience a wide range of trends that change over time. In the 1990s, it was considered fashionable for high school girls to wear very long and baggy “loose socks”, often pairing them with an extremely short skirt. It was around this same time that loose clothes were also popular with boys, who aimed to imitate American rappers by wearing oversized shirts and pants. These days, the current trend is for pastel colors and slim-fit designs that create a mo",

                "url": "https://livejapan.com/en/in-tokyo/in-pref-tokyo/in-tokyo_suburbs/article-a0002110/",
                "text": "\nThere, Japanese women wore the latest fashion from Paris while indulging in Western ballroom dancing, French cuisine, and more. However, Western style was limited to the women of Japan's high society during that time.   \n\nFollowing that, a style that blended Japanese and Western aesthetics arose, pairing Western-style accessories with authentically Japanese kimono.   \n\nEventually, around 1920, girls' school uniforms made the full change from hakama to Western fashion. Especially sailor-inspired outfits enjoyed great popularity among female students.  \n\nThe modern sailor uniform gained popularity as it pushed other styles aside. During this time, the Japanese uniforms weren't ready-made garments, but people had to make them at their own expense. If you had the 【136†fabric】 and pattern at hand, making a sailor-style uniform was relatively easy – another reason for its popularity.\n\n[Image 5: Left: early sailor-style uniform / Right: early Western-style uniform]\n\nLeft: early sailor-style uniform / Right: early Western-style uniform\n\n##  Experimenting with Different Japanese Fashion Styles\n\nAccording to Tomoko 【69†Namba】, girls have tried to wear their uniforms in cute or cool ways since the very beginning.  \n\n\"The school uniform started as a symbol of the elite, so just by wearing one, you were sure to attract admiration and envious glances. Besides, the uniform regulations were not strict before the war, so it was possible to use nice 【136†fabric】 and to adjust it to your own body shape, or style it in a way you like.\"\n\n[Image 6: Even today, a lot of Japanese middle school uniforms have a high collar and are in the sailor style. However, there are also schools that switch to blazers, as many go abroad for school trips and the high collar looks too much like a military uniform.]\n\nEven today, a lot of Japanese middle school uniforms have a high collar and are in the sailor style. However, there are also schools that switch to blazers, as many go abroad for school trips and the high collar looks too much like a military uniform.\n\n\"From the 1920s, girls' uniforms sported a shorter top combined with a long skirt. If the students didn't like the standard style, they seemed to have changed it to a more fashionable version. At that time, pleated skirts were en vogue. Even at schools that had regulations for how many folds a skirt needs to have, records 【137†show】 that the students would wear the small pleated style on days without uniform inspection.\"\n\n##  Sukeban – The “Delinquent Girl” Changes the Japanese School Uniform\n\n[Image 7: A modified school uniform. There were extreme trends such as flamboyant linings of boys’ collars, very wide pants or particularly short sailor-style tops, very long skirts, and so on.]\n\nA modified school uniform. There were extreme trends such as flamboyant linings of boys’ collars, very wide pants or particularly short sailor-style tops, very long skirts, and so on.\n\nThe background of these changes was the sukeban boom. The term sukeban is composed of the Japanese words for \"female\" and \"juvenile gang leader.\" It means just that – girl gangster boss. The male version is called tsuppari or banchō.   \n\nThese gang bosses were known for their daring disregard of school regulations and rules, as well as general bad behavior, such as threatening other students, not paying attention in lessons, and even organizing groups to gain influence and power. Proper ruffians, so to say.  \n\nThe looks played an important part as well. Hair dye and perm were common for both genders, these \"delinquent girls\" also wore heavy makeup, and uniforms were changed entirely to create an intimidating look, like the one shown in the picture above.   \n\nThe student bag that went with an outfit like that was often wrangled to such a flat shape that it was practically impossible to put anything inside. It represented the sukeban's anti-stance towards the school and its strict rules perfectly.  \n\nFrom a regular student's viewpoint, the sukeban style was intimidating yet also cool and somewhat fascinating. It was a time in which kids were under a lot of social pressure, fiercely competitive entrance exams being only one example, so the rebellious and bold sukeban seemed to strike a chord with many students.   \n\nBoth sukeban and tsuppari became somewhat of a phenomenon, bringing forth movies, books, 【138†manga】, bands, and even a cat character who wore a sukeban-style uniform was created – and they all were massive hits.  \n\nThe behavior of these girls and boys got more extreme, and eventually, violence at school and juvenile delinquency became a problem for Japanese society. \"Depravity starts with the clothes,\" was a phrase used by the Metropolitan Police Department, alongside an explanation of the sukeban style and a guide on how to identify said depravity in someone's outfit.   \n\nIt was such a significant topic, even the American magazine Newsweek picked it up. To combat this trend, both the school uniform industry and the schools themselves established clothing standards and enforced much stricter rules. It's an era with many regulations more stringent than the decades before.\n\n##  The Birth of Designer Brand Uniforms\n\nA direct result of these strict rules was the movement to abolish Japanese school uniforms altogether, stressing personal freedom and individuality.   \n\nIt was also the time when uniforms started to put value on looking stylish and switched to the now-popular blazer style, which has been thought of as rather unfashionable until then.   \n\nIn the wake of a declining birth rate, schools used stylish uniforms to attract students more aggressively and the clothes became a symbol of a school’s charm.  \n\nThis fashion-oriented strategy hit the mark just perfectly – the uniform you see in the image below created a flood of applications for its school.   \n\nIt was created ",

            {
              "start_ix": 278,
              "end_ix": 289,
              "citation_format_type": "tether_og",
              "metadata": {
                "type": "webpage",
                "title": "From Tradition to Today: Japanese School Uniforms",
                "url": "https://learnjapanese123.com/japanese-school-uniforms/",
                "text": "\nDepending on the discipline level of any particular school, students may often wear different seasonal and activity uniforms within the same classroom during the day. Certain schools also regulate student hairstyles, footwear, piercings, and book bags, but these particular rules have become slightly more relaxed in the modern times, and at some schools are only adhered to on special occasions such as school opening/closing ceremonies or school photo days.\n\nIt is normal for uniforms to be worn outside of school areas, and you will often see students out in groups in the evenings and on the weekends still dressed in their school uniforms. As for the younger kids, although not many public elementary schools in Japan require uniforms, many private schools and public schools run by the central government still do so.\n\n[Image 12][Image 13]\n\nSource: https://www.kuropixel.com/japanese-and-american-school-differences/\n\n## Girls’ Uniforms\n\nWhen you think of Japanese schoolgirls, you likely picture them in sailor uniforms. Seira fuku (セーラー服 ) is a common style of uniform worn by female middle school students, occasionally by high school students, and even rarely by elementary school students. It was first introduced as a school uniform in 1920 in Heian Jogakuin (平安女学院) and 1921 by the principal of Fukuoka Jo Gakuin University (福岡女学院), Elizabeth Lee. The sailor design was modeled after the uniform that was bring used by the British Royal Navy at the time, which Elizabeth Lee had seen firsthand when she had been an exchange student in the United Kingdom.\n\nThe uniform typically consists of a blouse with a sailor-style collar and a pleated skirt. As mentioned, the uniforms change slightly in the summer summer and winter, so depending on the season, the sleeve length and fabric are adjusted accordingly. A ribbon is tied in the front and laced through a loop attached to the blouse. Although seira fuku does all generally follow a similar design, the ribbon collar can vary, sometimes instead having neckties, bolo ties, neckerchiefs, and bows. The most common colors are navy blue, white, grey, and black.\n\nThe shoes, socks, and other accessories are sometimes included as part of the school’s regulated uniform. The socks are typically navy or white, and usually come up quite high. Shoes are generally brown or black penny loafers.\n\nToday, the sailor uniform is usually associated with junior high schools, since a majority of high schools have adapted the more Western-style plaid skirts or blazers.\n\n[Image 14][Image 15]\n\n[Image 16][Image 17]\n\n## Boys’ Uniforms\n\nJapanese boys at most public and private schools in Japan wear unique uniforms as well. By far, the most common uniform for boys is the gakuran (学ラン). It consists of a square-shouldered top with a stand-up collar, five golden buttons, and black straight-legged pants.  The color of the uniform is normally black, but some schools use navy blue.\n\nThe gakuran is derived from the Waffenrock, a military uniform that was introduced to Prussia in 1842. The term gakuran is a combination of gaku (学) meaning “study” or “student”, and ran (らん or 蘭) meaning the Netherlands or, historically in Japan, the West in general. Therefore, gakuran literally translates as “Western student”.\n\nThe uniform has a standing collar top that buttons down from top-to-bottom, and straight-legged pants that are worn with a dark-colored belt. As for the shoes, boys usually wear penny loafers or sneakers with this uniform. Some schools may require the students to wear collar-pins representing the school and/or class rank. Traditionally, the gakuran was also worn along with a matching (usually black) cap, but this custom is less common in modern times.\n\nThe buttons, typically gold-colored, are usually decorated with the school emblem. The second button is the one closest to the heart, and it’s common practice for a boy to give the second button from the top of a his uniform to a female he is in love with, as a sort of confession. This practice was apparently made popular by a scene in a novel by Taijun Takeda.\n\n[Image 18][Image 19]\n\n[Image 20][Image 21]\n\n## Modern Styles & Trends\n\nWhile these seira fuku and gakuran styles of uniforms are still quite common today, many schools these days have moved to more Western-pattern catholic school uniforms. These uniforms consist of a white shirt, a tie, a blazer with the school crest, and tailored trousers for the boys and a plaid skirt with high socks for the girls.\n\nThese days, various schools are known for their particular uniforms. For former students, school uniforms can have a nostalgic characteristic, and are often associated with relatively carefree youth. For current present-day students, uniforms are sometimes modified as a means of exhibiting individualism. Students may try to create their own trends by shortening the skirt, removing the ribbon, wearing small patches or badges under the collar, or even wearing subtle amounts of makeup. However, this isn’t just a current trend – there have always been young people who have sought to express a little originality by bending school uniform rules.\n\nJust as with mainstream fashion, student school uniforms experience a wide range of trends that change over time. In the 1990s, it was considered fashionable for high school girls to wear very long and baggy “loose socks”, often pairing them with an extremely short skirt. It was around this same time that loose clothes were also popular with boys, who aimed to imitate American rappers by wearing oversized shirts and pants. These days, the current trend is for pastel colors and slim-fit designs that create a mo",

              "snippet": "\nDepending on the discipline level of any particular school, students may often wear different seasonal and activity uniforms within the same classroom during the day. Certain schools also regulate student hairstyles, footwear, piercings, and book bags, but these particular rules have become slightly more relaxed in the modern times, and at some schools are only adhered to on special occasions such as school opening/closing ceremonies or school photo days.\n\nIt is normal for uniforms to be worn outside of school areas, and you will often see students out in groups in the evenings and on the weekends still dressed in their school uniforms. As for the younger kids, although not many public elementary schools in Japan require uniforms, many private schools and public schools run by the central government still do so.\n\n[Image 12][Image 13]\n\nSource: https://www.kuropixel.com/japanese-and-american-school-differences/\n\n## Girls’ Uniforms\n\nWhen you think of Japanese schoolgirls, you likely picture them in sailor uniforms. Seira fuku (セーラー服 ) is a common style of uniform worn by female middle school students, occasionally by high school students, and even rarely by elementary school students. It was first introduced as a school uniform in 1920 in Heian Jogakuin (平安女学院) and 1921 by the principal of Fukuoka Jo Gakuin University (福岡女学院), Elizabeth Lee. The sailor design was modeled after the uniform that was bring used by the British Royal Navy at the time, which Elizabeth Lee had seen firsthand when she had been an exchange student in the United Kingdom.\n\nThe uniform typically consists of a blouse with a sailor-style collar and a pleated skirt. As mentioned, the uniforms change slightly in the summer summer and winter, so depending on the season, the sleeve length and fabric are adjusted accordingly. A ribbon is tied in the front and laced through a loop attached to the blouse. Although seira fuku does all generally follow a similar design, the ribbon collar can vary, sometimes instead having neckties, bolo ties, neckerchiefs, and bows. The most common colors are navy blue, white, grey, and black.\n\nThe shoes, socks, and other accessories are sometimes included as part of the school’s regulated uniform. The socks are typically navy or white, and usually come up quite high. Shoes are generally brown or black penny loafers.\n\nToday, the sailor uniform is usually associated with junior high schools, since a majority of high schools have adapted the more Western-style plaid skirts or blazers.\n\n[Image 14][Image 15]\n\n[Image 16][Image 17]\n\n## Boys’ Uniforms\n\nJapanese boys at most public and private schools in Japan wear unique uniforms as well. By far, the most common uniform for boys is the gakuran (学ラン). It consists of a square-shouldered top with a stand-up collar, five golden buttons, and black straight-legged pants.  The color of the uniform is normally black, but some schools use navy blue.\n\nThe gakuran is derived from the Waffenrock, a military uniform that was introduced to Prussia in 1842. The term gakuran is a combination of gaku (学) meaning “study” or “student”, and ran (らん or 蘭) meaning the Netherlands or, historically in Japan, the West in general. Therefore, gakuran literally translates as “Western student”.\n\nThe uniform has a standing collar top that buttons down from top-to-bottom, and straight-legged pants that are worn with a dark-colored belt. As for the shoes, boys usually wear penny loafers or sneakers with this uniform. Some schools may require the students to wear collar-pins representing the school and/or class rank. Traditionally, the gakuran was also worn along with a matching (usually black) cap, but this custom is less common in modern times.\n\nThe buttons, typically gold-colored, are usually decorated with the school emblem. The second button is the one closest to the heart, and it’s common practice for a boy to give the second button from the top of a his uniform to a female he is in love with, as a sort of confession. This practice was apparently made popular by a scene in a novel by Taijun Takeda.\n\n[Image 18][Image 19]\n\n[Image 20][Image 21]\n\n## Modern Styles & Trends\n\nWhile these seira fuku and gakuran styles of uniforms are still quite common today, many schools these days have moved to more Western-pattern catholic school uniforms. These uniforms consist of a white shirt, a tie, a blazer with the school crest, and tailored trousers for the boys and a plaid skirt with high socks for the girls.\n\nThese days, various schools are known for their particular uniforms. For former students, school uniforms can have a nostalgic characteristic, and are often associated with relatively carefree youth. For current present-day students, uniforms are sometimes modified as a means of exhibiting individualism. Students may try to create their own trends by shortening the skirt, removing the ribbon, wearing small patches or badges under the collar, or even wearing subtle amounts of makeup. However, this isn’t just a current trend – there have always been young people who have sought to express a little originality by bending school uniform rules.\n\nJust as with mainstream fashion, student school uniforms experience a wide range of trends that change over time. In the 1990s, it was considered fashionable for high school girls to wear very long and baggy “loose socks”, often pairing them with an extremely short skirt. It was around this same time that loose clothes were also popular with boys, who aimed to imitate American rappers by wearing oversized shirts and pants. These days, the current trend is for pastel colors and slim-fit designs that create a mo",

            }

          "content_type": "text",
          "parts": [
            "熊本県宇土市の「あじさいの湯」について詳しく紹介します。\n\n### 基本情報\n- **住所**: 熊本県宇土市浦田町51\n- **電話番号**: 0964-23-4141\n- **営業時間**: 10:00～22:00\n- **休館日**: 毎月第2・第4火曜日\n\n### 施設概要\n「あじさいの湯」は、地下900メートルから湧き出す100%天然のアルカリ性単純温泉を使用しており、肌に優しい泉質が特徴です。大浴場、露天風呂、サウナ、家族風呂など、多彩な入浴施設が整っています。\n\n### 主な設備\n1. **大浴場**: 広々とした大浴場では、ゆったりとした入浴が楽しめます。\n2. **露天風呂**: 自然の中でリラックスできる露天風呂があります。\n3. **サウナ**: 高温サウナで汗を流すことができます。\n4. **家族風呂**: プライベートな空間で家族や友人と一緒に楽しめる家族風呂があります。\n5. **足湯**: 足だけを温める足湯も完備されています。\n\n### その他のサービス\n- **リラクゼーション施設**: マッサージやエステなどのリラクゼーションサービスも提供されています。\n- **食事処**: 地元の食材を使った料理を楽しめるレストランがあります。\n- **お土産コーナー**: 温泉地ならではのお土産を購入できるコーナーもあります。\n\n### アクセス\n- **車**: 熊本市内から約30分の距離に位置しています。駐車場も完備されており、車でのアクセスが便利です。\n- **公共交通機関**: JR宇土駅からタクシーで約10分。\n\n### 特色\n「あじさいの湯」は、豊かな自然に囲まれた環境で、静かにリラックスできる温泉地として地元の人々や観光客に愛されています。特に美肌効果が期待できる泉質で、女性にも人気があります。\n\n以上が、熊本県宇土市の「あじさいの湯」の詳細情報です。"
          ]
        },

          "domain": "yuru-to.net",
          "text": "\nURL: https://yuru-to.net/detail.php?oid=11731\n\n# 宇土市健康福祉館 あじさいの湯\n\n【0†ゆる～と】  > 【1†全国】  > 【2†熊本県】  > 【3†宇土市】  >  【4† 施設 】 共同浴場・公営浴場 \n\n  住所 熊本県宇土市網津町2283  \nTEL【5†0964-24-3456†tel:0964-24-3456】  \n営業時間 10:00〜21:00  \n定休日 第1・3月曜日   \n料金 大人 400 円 小人 300 円  \n最寄り   \nバス停 ルート\n\nあじさいの湯 \n\nから110m（徒歩2分）  \n最寄り駅 ルート\n\n住吉駅 \n\nから1.7km（徒歩21分）  \n泉質 アルカリ性単純温泉  \n効能 神経痛、筋肉痛、冷え性、疲労回復など  \n※営業時間や料金などに変更がある場合があります。施設のHPや電話で確認してからご利用ください。  \n  天然温泉 [Image 0] 休憩所 [Image 1]  \n露天風呂 [Image 2] 食事処 [Image 3]  \nサウナ [Image 4] 駐車場 30台  \n\n〖広告〗割引・予約 \n\n[Image 5]\n\nじゃらん\n\n[Image 6]\n\nJAF\n\n[Image 7]\n\nアソビュー\n\nJAFへの入会はこちら[Image 8]\n\n[Image 9]ホームページを開く\n\n[Image 10]Googleプレイスを開く\n\n▲クチコミや写真はこちら\n\n[Image 11]Googleで検索する\n\n[Image 12]サウナイキタイから探す\n\n[Image 13]ウェザーニュースの天気\n\n[Image 14]施設へのルートを調べる\n\n[Image 15]誤りを指摘する\n\n[Image 16]空から俯瞰する\n\nGoogleクチコミ情報\n\n3.7 \n\n255件\n\n[Image 17]\n\n  [Image 18]【6†tsuru 3†www.google.com】  \n 2024/5/17   \n市営の施設？シャンプー・ボディソープ設置されていますが入浴料が安いです。  \n湯船は十分広いですが洗い場が７脚なので空いている時間に利用するのが良いかと。\n\n更に見る  \n\n[Image 19]【7†おいちゃん!熊本で単身赴任中†www.google.com】  \n 2024/1/8   \n地下900mから湧く、100%天然のアルカリ性単純温泉。  \nサウナ、水風呂、打たせ湯あり。露天風呂なし。シャンプー、ボディソープあり。地元の方ばかりで、皆さん「やあ、久しぶり」「お先です」と、ごあいさつしてます。市外の方400円（大人）、市内の方300円（同）。  \n貴重品のロッカーは、100円（靴箱の横、返金あり）と、10円（脱衣所）。  \n[Image 20]【8†肥後biker†www.google.com】  \n 2024/1/2   \n市外在住者は400円、シャンプー、ボディーソープ備え付け有り。ドライヤー有ります、ロッカーは10円必要で小さめです。  \n浴場は大きめの内湯と小さめの低温湯があります。露天に寝そべり湯（使用不可中）打たせ湯（冷水）、湯船はありません、サウナも外にあります。  \nお湯は若干、とろみのあるお湯で無臭、湯温は丁度いい熱さ（恐らく41℃ほど）でした。  \n休憩所は広く、長めのソファやテーブル席が有り、休憩室もありました。  \n[Image 21]【9†17 K,s†www.google.com】  \n 2023/6/5   \n家族で初入浴。宇土市外は、100円たかいがそれでも400円は、安い。大人2名、子供1名なのだが、家族割との事で、100円引きとの事で、チケットを大人1枚、小人2枚で購入して下さいと言われました。結果100円引きだが、ちょっと複雑（笑）。お風呂は、露天風呂は、無いが、サウナや、水風呂もあり、満足。またサウナに小さいがテレビもあるので、長居してしまう。地元の方が多いので、サウナは、一部の方が場所取りや、大声で話しているのでマナーは、悪い。水風呂は、推定20度位なので、爽快感が足りない。この時季は、名前の通り、アジサイがいっぱい咲いていて、子供も喜んでました。  \n[Image 22]【10†けんKen†www.google.com】  \n 2022/8/1   \n駐車場は   \n施設の規模の割に、結構広目のイメージ。(嬉しい)  \n\n入湯料も   \n宇土市外の大人でも…  \n400円/人とは、これまた嬉しい。( ^o^)  \n\n・シャンプー  \n・ボディーソープ設置有り。  \n\nお客さんは  \n“地元のご年配の方々“が大半ですかねー。  \n\n脱衣場が   \nモーちょっと広ければ   \n使い易いかなぁー(￣∇￣)  \n\n施設の事ではありませんが…  \n\n温泉を利用後の帰り道で   \nJR：三角線を渡って   \nR：57を宇土市内方面に行こうとすると…  \n\n・時間帯に依っては57号線の車が途切れない。  \n・三角線の踏み切り内でで車が途切れるのを待ってると…  \n列車到来の「キンコン音」と遮断機が降下。  \n\n結～構   \n焦りますので要注意です。(;^_^A  \n\n周辺の入浴施設（半径10km以内・近い順）\n\n  種別 施設名 料金 設備 住所 交通  \n[Image 23]【11†あじさいの湯 足湯】無料 \n\n天然温泉 \n\n露天 \n\nサウナ \n\n休憩所 \n\n食事処 \n\n30台 \n\n宇土市網津町2283  \n[Image 24]【12†天明老人福祉センター】¥100 \n\n天然温泉 \n\n露天 \n\nサウナ \n\n休憩所 \n\n食事処 \n\n駐車場 \n\n熊本市南区銭塘町2172  \n[Image 25]【13†しあわせの湯】¥600 \n\n天然温泉 \n\n露天 \n\nサウナ \n\n休憩所 \n\n食事処 \n\n50台 \n\n熊本市南区富合町田尻611  \n[Image 26]【14†富合サウナランド】¥600 \n\n天然温泉 \n\n露天 \n\nサウナ \n\n休憩所 \n\n食事処 \n\n15台 \n\n熊本市南区富合町田尻45-1  \n[Image 27]【15†南老人福祉センター】¥100 \n\n天然温泉 \n\n露天 \n\nサウナ \n\n休憩所 \n\n食事処 \n\n駐車場 \n\n熊本市南区川尻4-8-13  \n[Image 28]【16†冨乃湯】¥450 \n\n天然温泉 \n\n露天 \n\nサウナ \n\n休憩所 \n\n食事処 \n\n駐車場 \n\n熊本市南区川尻4-10-20  \n\n※情報が古くなっている場合があります。各施設のHPや電話でご確認の上、ご利用するようにお願いいたします。 \n\n© 2018-2024 ゆる～と             "熊本県宇土市の「あじさいの湯」は、宇土市が運営する健康福祉施設です。この施設は、地下900メートルから湧き出す100%天然のアルカリ性単純温泉を特徴とし、地元住民だけでなく観光客にも人気があります。\n\n### 基本情報\n- **住所**: 熊本県宇土市網津町2283\n- **電話番号**: 0964-24-3456\n- **営業時間**: 10:00～21:00\n- **定休日**: 第1・第3月曜日、および12月31日～1月1日\n\n### 施設の特徴\n- **温泉**: 広々とした大浴場、サウナ、打たせ湯があります。露天風呂はありませんが、清潔で快適な内湯を楽しむことができます。\n- **泉質**: アルカリ性単純温泉で、神経痛、筋肉痛、冷え性、疲労回復などに効果があるとされています。\n- **料金**: 大人400円（市民300円）、子供300円（市民200円）\n- **設備**: シャンプーやボディソープが備え付けられており、ドライヤーも利用可能です。貴重品ロッカーも完備されています。\n\n### アクセス\n- **車**: 九州自動車道松橋ICから国道218号・3号・57号を経由して約15km\n- **公共交通機関**: JR三角線の住吉駅から徒歩約15分、またはタクシーで約3分\n\n### その他のサービス\n- **休憩所**: 広々とした休憩スペースがあり、リラックスするのに適しています。\n- **食事処**: 地元の食材を使用した食事が楽しめます。\n\n### 運営\n「あじさいの湯」は市営の健康福祉施設であり、住民の健康増進とリラクゼーションを目的としています【32†source】【33†source】。\n\n詳細な情報や最新の営業状況については、公式ウェブサイトをご確認ください: [宇土市健康福祉館 あじさいの湯](https://www.city.uto.lg.jp/article/view/1092/1774.html)"
          ]
        },
        "status": "finished_successfully",

                "text": "\nURL: https://yuru-to.net/detail.php?oid=11731\n\n# 宇土市健康福祉館 あじさいの湯\n\n【0†ゆる～と】  > 【1†全国】  > 【2†熊本県】  > 【3†宇土市】  >  【4† 施設 】 共同浴場・公営浴場 \n\n  住所 熊本県宇土市網津町2283  \nTEL【5†0964-24-3456†tel:0964-24-3456】  \n営業時間 10:00〜21:00  \n定休日 第1・3月曜日   \n料金 大人 400 円 小人 300 円  \n最寄り   \nバス停 ルート\n\nあじさいの湯 \n\nから110m（徒歩2分）  \n最寄り駅 ルート\n\n住吉駅 \n\nから1.7km（徒歩21分）  \n泉質 アルカリ性単純温泉  \n効能 神経痛、筋肉痛、冷え性、疲労回復など  \n※営業時間や料金などに変更がある場合があります。施設のHPや電話で確認してからご利用ください。  \n  天然温泉 [Image 0] 休憩所 [Image 1]  \n露天風呂 [Image 2] 食事処 [Image 3]  \nサウナ [Image 4] 駐車場 30台  \n\n〖広告〗割引・予約 \n\n[Image 5]\n\nじゃらん\n\n[Image 6]\n\nJAF\n\n[Image 7]\n\nアソビュー\n\nJAFへの入会はこちら[Image 8]\n\n[Image 9]ホームページを開く\n\n[Image 10]Googleプレイスを開く\n\n▲クチコミや写真はこちら\n\n[Image 11]Googleで検索する\n\n[Image 12]サウナイキタイから探す\n\n[Image 13]ウェザーニュースの天気\n\n[Image 14]施設へのルートを調べる\n\n[Image 15]誤りを指摘する\n\n[Image 16]空から俯瞰する\n\nGoogleクチコミ情報\n\n3.7 \n\n255件\n\n[Image 17]\n\n  [Image 18]【6†tsuru 3†www.google.com】  \n 2024/5/17   \n市営の施設？シャンプー・ボディソープ設置されていますが入浴料が安いです。  \n湯船は十分広いですが洗い場が７脚なので空いている時間に利用するのが良いかと。\n\n更に見る  \n\n[Image 19]【7†おいちゃん!熊本で単身赴任中†www.google.com】  \n 2024/1/8   \n地下900mから湧く、100%天然のアルカリ性単純温泉。  \nサウナ、水風呂、打たせ湯あり。露天風呂なし。シャンプー、ボディソープあり。地元の方ばかりで、皆さん「やあ、久しぶり」「お先です」と、ごあいさつしてます。市外の方400円（大人）、市内の方300円（同）。  \n貴重品のロッカーは、100円（靴箱の横、返金あり）と、10円（脱衣所）。  \n[Image 20]【8†肥後biker†www.google.com】  \n 2024/1/2   \n市外在住者は400円、シャンプー、ボディーソープ備え付け有り。ドライヤー有ります、ロッカーは10円必要で小さめです。  \n浴場は大きめの内湯と小さめの低温湯があります。露天に寝そべり湯（使用不可中）打たせ湯（冷水）、湯船はありません、サウナも外にあります。  \nお湯は若干、とろみのあるお湯で無臭、湯温は丁度いい熱さ（恐らく41℃ほど）でした。  \n休憩所は広く、長めのソファやテーブル席が有り、休憩室もありました。  \n[Image 21]【9†17 K,s†www.google.com】  \n 2023/6/5   \n家族で初入浴。宇土市外は、100円たかいがそれでも400円は、安い。大人2名、子供1名なのだが、家族割との事で、100円引きとの事で、チケットを大人1枚、小人2枚で購入して下さいと言われました。結果100円引きだが、ちょっと複雑（笑）。お風呂は、露天風呂は、無いが、サウナや、水風呂もあり、満足。またサウナに小さいがテレビもあるので、長居してしまう。地元の方が多いので、サウナは、一部の方が場所取りや、大声で話しているのでマナーは、悪い。水風呂は、推定20度位なので、爽快感が足りない。この時季は、名前の通り、アジサイがいっぱい咲いていて、子供も喜んでました。  \n[Image 22]【10†けんKen†www.google.com】  \n 2022/8/1   \n駐車場は   \n施設の規模の割に、結構広目のイメージ。(嬉しい)  \n\n入湯料も   \n宇土市外の大人でも…  \n400円/人とは、これまた嬉しい。( ^o^)  \n\n・シャンプー  \n・ボディーソープ設置有り。  \n\nお客さんは  \n“地元のご年配の方々“が大半ですかねー。  \n\n脱衣場が   \nモーちょっと広ければ   \n使い易いかなぁー(￣∇￣)  \n\n施設の事ではありませんが…  \n\n温泉を利用後の帰り道で   \nJR：三角線を渡って   \nR：57を宇土市内方面に行こうとすると…  \n\n・時間帯に依っては57号線の車が途切れない。  \n・三角線の踏み切り内でで車が途切れるのを待ってると…  \n列車到来の「キンコン音」と遮断機が降下。  \n\n結～構   \n焦りますので要注意です。(;^_^A  \n\n周辺の入浴施設（半径10km以内・近い順）\n\n  種別 施設名 料金 設備 住所 交通  \n[Image 23]【11†あじさいの湯 足湯】無料 \n\n天然温泉 \n\n露天 \n\nサウナ \n\n休憩所 \n\n食事処 \n\n30台 \n\n宇土市網津町2283               "snippet": "\nURL: https://yuru-to.net/detail.php?oid=11731\n\n# 宇土市健康福祉館 あじさいの湯\n\n【0†ゆる～と】  > 【1†全国】  > 【2†熊本県】  > 【3†宇土市】  >  【4† 施設 】 共同浴場・公営浴場 \n\n  住所 熊本県宇土市網津町2283  \nTEL【5†0964-24-3456†tel:0964-24-3456】  \n営業時間 10:00〜21:00  \n定休日 第1・3月曜日   \n料金 大人 400 円 小人 300 円  \n最寄り   \nバス停 ルート\n\nあじさいの湯 \n\nから110m（徒歩2分）  \n最寄り駅 ルート\n\n住吉駅 \n\nから1.7km（徒歩21分）  \n泉質 アルカリ性単純温泉  \n効能 神経痛、筋肉痛、冷え性、疲労回復など  \n※営業時間や料金などに変更がある場合があります。施設のHPや電話で確認してからご利用ください。  \n  天然温泉 [Image 0] 休憩所 [Image 1]  \n露天風呂 [Image 2] 食事処 [Image 3]  \nサウナ [Image 4] 駐車場 30台  \n\n〖広告〗割引・予約 \n\n[Image 5]\n\nじゃらん\n\n[Image 6]\n\nJAF\n\n[Image 7]\n\nアソビュー\n\nJAFへの入会はこちら[Image 8]\n\n[Image 9]ホームページを開く\n\n[Image 10]Googleプレイスを開く\n\n▲クチコミや写真はこちら\n\n[Image 11]Googleで検索する\n\n[Image 12]サウナイキタイから探す\n\n[Image 13]ウェザーニュースの天気\n\n[Image 14]施設へのルートを調べる\n\n[Image 15]誤りを指摘する\n\n[Image 16]空から俯瞰する\n\nGoogleクチコミ情報\n\n3.7 \n\n255件\n\n[Image 17]\n\n  [Image 18]【6†tsuru 3†www.google.com】  \n 2024/5/17   \n市営の施設？シャンプー・ボディソープ設置されていますが入浴料が安いです。  \n湯船は十分広いですが洗い場が７脚なので空いている時間に利用するのが良いかと。\n\n更に見る  \n\n[Image 19]【7†おいちゃん!熊本で単身赴任中†www.google.com】  \n 2024/1/8   \n地下900mから湧く、100%天然のアルカリ性単純温泉。  \nサウナ、水風呂、打たせ湯あり。露天風呂なし。シャンプー、ボディソープあり。地元の方ばかりで、皆さん「やあ、久しぶり」「お先です」と、ごあいさつしてます。市外の方400円（大人）、市内の方300円（同）。  \n貴重品のロッカーは、100円（靴箱の横、返金あり）と、10円（脱衣所）。  \n[Image 20]【8†肥後biker†www.google.com】  \n 2024/1/2   \n市外在住者は400円、シャンプー、ボディーソープ備え付け有り。ドライヤー有ります、ロッカーは10円必要で小さめです。  \n浴場は大きめの内湯と小さめの低温湯があります。露天に寝そべり湯（使用不可中）打たせ湯（冷水）、湯船はありません、サウナも外にあります。  \nお湯は若干、とろみのあるお湯で無臭、湯温は丁度いい熱さ（恐らく41℃ほど）でした。  \n休憩所は広く、長めのソファやテーブル席が有り、休憩室もありました。  \n[Image 21]【9†17 K,s†www.google.com】  \n 2023/6/5   \n家族で初入浴。宇土市外は、100円たかいがそれでも400円は、安い。大人2名、子供1名なのだが、家族割との事で、100円引きとの事で、チケットを大人1枚、小人2枚で購入して下さいと言われました。結果100円引きだが、ちょっと複雑（笑）。お風呂は、露天風呂は、無いが、サウナや、水風呂もあり、満足。またサウナに小さいがテレビもあるので、長居してしまう。地元の方が多いので、サウナは、一部の方が場所取りや、大声で話しているのでマナーは、悪い。水風呂は、推定20度位なので、爽快感が足りない。この時季は、名前の通り、アジサイがいっぱい咲いていて、子供も喜んでました。  \n[Image 22]【10†けんKen†www.google.com】  \n 2022/8/1   \n駐車場は   \n施設の規模の割に、結構広目のイメージ。(嬉しい)  \n\n入湯料も   \n宇土市外の大人でも…  \n400円/人とは、これまた嬉しい。( ^o^)  \n\n・シャンプー  \n・ボディーソープ設置有り。  \n\nお客さんは  \n“地元のご年配の方々“が大半ですかねー。  \n\n脱衣場が   \nモーちょっと広ければ   \n使い易いかなぁー(￣∇￣)  \n\n施設の事ではありませんが…  \n\n温泉を利用後の帰り道で   \nJR：三角線を渡って   \nR：57を宇土市内方面に行こうとすると…  \n\n・時間帯に依っては57号線の車が途切れない。  \n・三角線の踏み切り内でで車が途切れるのを待ってると…  \n列車到来の「キンコン音」と遮断機が降下。  \n\n結～構   \n焦りますので要注意です。(;^_^A  \n\n周辺の入浴施設（半径10km以内・近い順）\n\n  種別 施設名 料金 設備 住所 交通  \n[Image 23]【11†あじさいの湯 足湯】無料 \n\n天然温泉 \n\n露天 \n\nサウナ \n\n休憩所 \n\n食事処 \n\n30台 \n\n宇土市網津町2283           "text": "\n最低[前日差]\n\n23℃[+2]\n\n時間 0-6 6-12 12-18 18-24   \n降水確率 100 80 20 10  \n\n【77†降水確率・週間天気を見る】\n\n情報提供：\n\n[Image 10]\n\n口コミを書く 施設情報を送る\n\n【70†保存】\n\n1\n\n [Image 11] \n\n### おすすめ！ 親子のおでかけ特集 6月\n\n  * 【96†家族でジュージュー！ 手ぶらバーベキュー！】\n  * 【97†新緑シーズンのおでかけは 「家族キャンプ」へGO!!】\n  * 【98†アンケートに答えるだけで Amazonギフトカードが当たる】\n  * 【99†その道のプロに教わる こだわりの親子体験プログラム！】\n\n### 今週の注目TOPICS\n\n  * 【100†〖東京近郊〗2024年6月の親子向けおでかけまとめ†report.iko-yo.net】\n  * 【101†〖関西〗2024年6月の親子向けおでかけ先まとめ！†report.iko-yo.net】\n  * 【102†〖東海〗2024年6月の親子向けおでかけ先まとめ！†report.iko-yo.net】\n\n【48†記事をもっと見る†report.iko-yo.net】\n\n#### 九州･沖縄の親子のお出かけ人気スポットランキング \n\n##### 都道府県人気おでかけランキング\n\n  * 【36†九州･沖縄エリアのおでかけランキング】\n  * 【103†福岡県のおでかけランキング】\n  * 【104†佐賀県のおでかけランキング】\n  * 【105†長崎県のおでかけランキング】\n  * 【106†熊本県のおでかけランキング】\n  * 【107†大分県のおでかけランキング】\n  * 【108†宮崎県のおでかけランキング】\n  * 【109†鹿児島県のおでかけランキング】\n  * 【110†沖縄県のおでかけランキング】\n\n#### 熊本周辺の注目お出かけ\n\n  * 【111†〖キッザニア福岡〗年齢別の楽しみ方 福岡県福岡市博多区 年齢別におすすめのアクティビティをご紹介！】\n\n#### 熊本県宇土市 今日・明日の天気予報 \n\n予報地点：熊本県宇土市 \n\n2024年06月20日 06時00分 発表 \n\n6月20日(木)\n\n[Image 12]\n\n雨 \n\n24℃[-6]\n\n21℃[+1]\n\n6月21日(金)\n\n[Image 13]\n\n雨 のち くもり \n\n28℃[+4]\n\n23℃[+2]\n\n【112†降水確率・週間天気を見る】\n\n#### 必見! お出かけ家族にイチオシ情報\n\n  *   *   *   *   * \n\n#### いこーよとりっぷ 注目記事\n\n  * 【113†6月のおでかけにピッタリ 東京近郊の親子向けイベント！ 伝統あるお祭り＆花祭りなど†trip.iko-yo.net】\n  * 【114†今が見頃！関東・あじさいの名所 あじさいの人気スポット 1万株以上が咲き渡る絶景も！†trip.iko-yo.net】\n  * 【115†五感で自然を大満喫！ 美しい里山の自然や文化を体感！ 収穫体験やそば打ち体験も†trip.iko-yo.net】\n  * 【116†親子におすすめ♪古民家カフェ 関東の古民家カフェ＆レストラン 親子連れにおすすめの11選†trip.iko-yo.net】\n\n#### 大人気！ 親子のおでかけ特集 6月\n\n  * 【96†家族でジュージュー！ 手ぶらバーベキュー！】\n  * 【97†新緑シーズンのおでかけは 「家族キャンプ」へGO!!】\n  * 【98†アンケートに答えるだけで Amazonギフトカードが当たる】\n  * 【117†雨の日を楽しむ！ アクティブスポットガイド】\n  * 【99†その道のプロに教わる こだわりの親子体験プログラム！】\n\n【50†特集をもっと見る】\n\n#### 編集部オススメ「注目記事」\n\n  * 【100†[東京]6月のおでかけ先まとめ 泡＆水＆スライダーで大はしゃぎ 人気アイス食べ放題＆水遊びも♪†report.iko-yo.net】\n  * 【101†[関西]6月のおでかけ先まとめ 無料＆予約不要で工場見学！ 入場無料の親子イベントも†report.iko-yo.net】\n  * 【102†[東海]6月のおでかけ先まとめ トランポリンパークで大満足♪ 冷やし芋フェス＆ホタル鑑賞も†report.iko-yo.net】\n  * 【118†エビ・カニは何歳からOK？ 甲殻類アレルギーの特徴＆症状 注意したい子供の年齢も紹介！】\n  * 【119†〖2024年上映中〗子供向け映画 親子で観たい映画が続々公開！ あらすじ＆先取り情報をチェック】\n  * 【120†おさるの楽園に行こう 「おさるランド＆アニタウン」 ペンギンやカピバラのふれあいも†report.iko-yo.net】\n  * 【121†夏の水分補給は要注意！ 直飲みしたペットボトル、 何日後まで飲んでも大",

          "content_type": "tether_quote",
          "url": "https://yuru-to.net/detail.php?oid=11731",
          "domain": "yuru-to.net",
          "text": "\n[Image 12]サウナイキタイから探す\n\n[Image 13]ウェザーニュースの天気\n\n[Image 14]施設へのルートを調べる\n\n[Image 15]誤りを指摘する\n\n[Image 16]空から俯瞰する\n\nGoogleクチコミ情報\n\n3.7 \n\n255件\n\n[Image 17]\n\n  [Image 18]【6†tsuru 3†www.google.com】  \n 2024/5/17   \n市営の施設？シャンプー・ボディソープ設置されていますが入浴料が安いです。  \n湯船は十分広いですが洗い場が７脚なので空いている時間に利用するのが良いかと。\n\n更に見る  \n\n[Image 19]【7†おいちゃん!熊本で単身赴任中†www.google.com】  \n 2024/1/8   \n地下900mから湧く、100%天然のアルカリ性単純温泉。  \nサウナ、水風呂、打たせ湯あり。露天風呂なし。シャンプー、ボディソープあり。地元の方ばかりで、皆さん「やあ、久しぶり」「お先です」と、ごあいさつしてます。市外の方400円（大人）、市内の方300円（同）。  \n貴重品のロッカーは、100円（靴箱の横、返金あり）と、10円（脱衣所）。  \n[Image 20]【8†肥後biker†www.google.com】  \n 2024/1/2   \n市外在住者は400円、シャンプー、ボディーソープ備え付け有り。ドライヤー有ります、ロッカーは10円必要で小さめです。  \n浴場は大きめの内湯と小さめの低温湯があります。露天に寝そべり湯（使用不可中）打たせ湯（冷水）、湯船はありません、サウナも外にあります。  \nお湯は若干、とろみのあるお湯で無臭、湯温は丁度いい熱さ（恐らく41℃ほど）でした。  \n休憩所は広く、長めのソファやテーブル席が有り、休憩室もありました。  \n[Image 21]【9†17 K,s†www.google.com】  \n 2023/6/5   \n家族で初入浴。宇土市外は、100円たかいがそれでも400円は、安い。大人2名、子供1名なのだが、家族割との事で、100円引きとの事で、チケットを大人1枚、小人2枚で購入して下さいと言われました。結果100円引きだが、ちょっと複雑（笑）。お風呂は、露天風呂は、無いが、サウナや、水風呂もあり、満足。またサウナに小さいがテレビもあるので、長居してしまう。地元の方が多いので、サウナは、一部の方が場所取りや、大声で話しているのでマナーは、悪い。水風呂は、推定20度位なので、爽快感が足りない。この時季は、名前の通り、アジサイがいっぱい咲いていて、子供も喜んでました。  \n[Image 22]【10†けんKen†www.google.com】  \n 2022/8/1   \n駐車場は   \n施設の規模の割に、結構広目のイメージ。(嬉しい)  \n\n入湯料も   \n宇土市外の大人でも…  \n400円/人とは、これまた嬉しい。( ^o^)  \n\n・シャンプー  \n・ボディーソープ設置有り。  \n\nお客さんは  \n“地元のご年配の方々“が大半ですかねー。  \n\n脱衣場が   \nモーちょっと広ければ   \n使い易いかなぁー(￣∇￣)  \n\n施設の事ではありませんが…  \n\n温泉を利用後の帰り道で   \nJR：三角線を渡って   \nR：57を宇土市内方面に行こうとすると…  \n\n・時間帯に依っては57号線の車が途切れない。  \n・三角線の踏み切り内でで車が途切れるのを待ってると…  \n列車到来の「キンコン音」と遮断機が降下。  \n\n結～構   \n焦りますので要注意です。(;^_^A  \n\n周辺の入浴施設（半径10km以内・近い順）\n\n  種別 施設名 料金 設備 住所 交通  \n[Image 23]【11†あじさいの湯 足湯】無料 \n\n天然温泉 \n\n露天 \n\nサウナ \n\n休憩所 \n\n食事処 \n\n30台 \n\n宇土市網津町2283  \n[Image 24]【12†天明老人福祉センター】¥100 \n\n天然温泉 \n\n露天 \n\nサウナ \n\n休憩所 \n\n食事処 \n\n駐車場 \n\n熊本市南区銭塘町2172  \n[Image 25]【13†しあわせの湯】¥600 \n\n天然温泉 \n\n露天 \n\nサウナ \n\n休憩所 \n\n食事処 \n\n50台 \n\n熊本市南区富合町田尻611  \n[Image 26]【14†富合サウナランド】¥600 \n\n天然温泉 \n\n露天 \n\nサウナ \n\n休憩所 \n\n食事処 \n\n15台 \n\n熊本市南区富合町田尻45-1  \n[Image 27]【15†南老人福祉センター】¥100 \n\n天然温泉 \n\n露天 \n\nサウナ \n\n休憩所 \n\n食事処 \n\n駐車場 \n\n熊本市南区川",

\n地下900mから湧く、100%天然のアルカリ性単純温泉。  \nサウナ、水風呂、打たせ湯あり。露天風呂なし。シャンプー、ボディソープあり。地元の方ばかりで、皆さん「やあ、久しぶり」「お先です」と、ごあいさつしてます。市外の方400円（大人）、市内の方300円（同）。  \n貴重品のロッカーは、100円（靴箱の横、返金あり）と、10円（脱衣所）。  \n[Image 20]【8†肥後biker†www.google.com】  \n 2024/1/2   \n市外在住者は400円、シャンプー、ボディーソープ備え付け有り。ドライヤー有ります、ロッカーは10円必要で小さめです。  \n浴場は大きめの内湯と小さめの低温湯があります。露天に寝そべり湯（使用不可中）打たせ湯（冷水）、湯船はありません、サウナも外にあります。  \nお湯は若干、とろみのあるお湯で無臭、湯温は丁度いい熱さ（恐らく41℃ほど）でした。  \n休憩所は広く、長めのソファやテーブル席が有り、休憩室もありました。  \n[Image 21]【9†17 K,s†www.google.com】  \n 2023/6/5   \n家族で初入浴。宇土市外は、100円たかいがそれでも400円は、安い。大人2名、子供1名なのだが、家族割との事で、100円引きとの事で、チケットを大人1枚、小人2枚で購入して下さいと言われました。結果100円引きだが、ちょっと複雑（笑）。お風呂は、露天風呂は、無いが、サウナや、水風呂もあり、満足。またサウナに小さいがテレビもあるので、長居してしまう。地元の方が多いので、サウナは、一部の方が場所取りや、大声で話しているのでマナーは、悪い。水風呂は、推定20度位なので、爽快感が足りない。この時季は、名前の通り、アジサイがいっぱい咲いていて、子供も喜んでました。  \n[Image 22]【10†けんKen†www.google.com】  \n 2022/8/1   \n駐車場は   \n施設の規模の割に、結構広目のイメージ。(嬉しい)  \n\n入湯料も   \n宇土市外の大人でも…  \n400円/人とは、これまた嬉しい。( ^o^)  \n\n・シャンプー  \n・ボディーソープ設置有り。  \n\nお客さんは  \n“地元のご年配の方々“が大半ですかねー。  \n\n脱衣場が   \nモーちょっと広ければ   \n使い易いかなぁー(￣∇￣)  \n\n施設の事ではありませんが…  \n\n温泉を利用後の帰り道で   \nJR：三角線を渡って   \nR：57を宇土市内方面に行こうとすると…  \n\n・時間帯に依っては57号線の車が途切れない。  \n・三角線の踏み切り内でで車が途切れるのを待ってると…  \n列車到来の「キンコン音」と遮断機が降下。  \n\n結～構   \n焦りますので要注意です。(;^_^A  \n\n周辺の入浴施設（半径10km以内・近い順）\n\n  種別 施設名 料金 設備 住所 交通  \n[Image 23]【11†あじさいの湯 足湯】無料 \n\n天然温泉 \n\n露天 \n\nサウナ \n\n休憩所 \n\n食事処 \n\n30台 \n\n宇土市網津町2283           "content_type": "text",
          "parts": [
            "熊本県宇土市の「あじさいの湯」は、宇土市が運営する健康福祉施設であり、地元住民だけでなく観光客にも人気があります。この施設は、100%天然のアルカリ性単純温泉を特徴とし、広々とした大浴場やサウナなどの設備を備えています。\n\n### 施設の財務状況\n「あじさいの湯」は市営施設であるため、その運営は宇土市の予算内で行われています。入浴料金は市内外で異なり、市外からの訪問者は大人400円、市内の住民は300円となっています。また、子供の料金も設定されており、地域住民にとって利用しやすい価格設定がされています【32†source】【39†source】【41†source】。\n\n施設は市営であるため、収益は主に運営費の補填と施設の維持管理に充てられています。具体的な収支については、宇土市の予算報告書や決算書にて確認することができます。\n\n### 利用者の声\n多くの利用者からは、料金の手頃さや施設の清潔さ、地元住民との交流の場としての魅力が高く評価されています。ただし、設備面での改善点として、洗い場の数や脱衣所の広さについての指摘もあります【41†source】【42†source】。\n\n最新の運営状況や財務状況については、宇土市の公式ウェブサイトや市役所にお問い合わせいただくことをおすすめします。"
          ]
        },
        "status": "finished_successfully",
        "end_turn": true,

\n地下900mから湧く、100%天然のアルカリ性単純温泉。  \nサウナ、水風呂、打たせ湯あり。露天風呂なし。シャンプー、ボディソープあり。地元の方ばかりで、皆さん「やあ、久しぶり」「お先です」と、ごあいさつしてます。市外の方400円（大人）、市内の方300円（同）。  \n貴重品のロッカーは、100円（靴箱の横、返金あり）と、10円（脱衣所）。  \n[Image 20]【8†肥後biker†www.google.com】  \n 2024/1/2   \n市外在住者は400円、シャンプー、ボディーソープ備え付け有り。ドライヤー有ります、ロッカーは10円必要で小さめです。  \n浴場は大きめの内湯と小さめの低温湯があります。露天に寝そべり湯（使用不可中）打たせ湯（冷水）、湯船はありません、サウナも外にあります。  \nお湯は若干、とろみのあるお湯で無臭、湯温は丁度いい熱さ（恐らく41℃ほど）でした。  \n休憩所は広く、長めのソファやテーブル席が有り、休憩室もありました。  \n[Image 21]【9†17 K,s†www.google.com】  \n 2023/6/5   \n家族で初入浴。宇土市外は、100円たかいがそれでも400円は、安い。大人2名、子供1名なのだが、家族割との事で、100円引きとの事で、チケットを大人1枚、小人2枚で購入して下さいと言われました。結果100円引きだが、ちょっと複雑（笑）。お風呂は、露天風呂は、無いが、サウナや、水風呂もあり、満足。またサウナに小さいがテレビもあるので、長居してしまう。地元の方が多いので、サウナは、一部の方が場所取りや、大声で話しているのでマナーは、悪い。水風呂は、推定20度位なので、爽快感が足りない。この時季は、名前の通り、アジサイがいっぱい咲いていて、子供も喜んでました。  \n[Image 22]【10†けんKen†www.google.com】  \n 2022/8/1   \n駐車場は   \n施設の規模の割に、結構広目のイメージ。(嬉しい)  \n\n入湯料も   \n宇土市外の大人でも…  \n400円/人とは、これまた嬉しい。( ^o^)  \n\n・シャンプー  \n・ボディーソープ設置有り。  \n\nお客さんは               "snippet": "\n[Image 12]サウナイキタイから探す\n\n[Image 13]ウェザーニュースの天気\n\n[Image 14]施設へのルートを調べる\n\n[Image 15]誤りを指摘する\n\n[Image 16]空から俯瞰する\n\nGoogleクチコミ情報\n\n3.7 \n\n255件\n\n[Image 17]\n\n  [Image 18]【6†tsuru 3†www.google.com】  \n 2024/5/17   \n市営の施設？シャンプー・ボディソープ設置されていますが入浴料が安いです。  \n湯船は十分広いですが洗い場が７脚なので空いている時間に利用するのが良いかと。\n\n更に見る  \n\n[Image 19]【7†おいちゃん!熊本で単身赴任中†www.google.com】  \n 2024/1/8   \n地下900mから湧く、100%天然のアルカリ性単純温泉。  \nサウナ、水風呂、打たせ湯あり。露天風呂なし。シャンプー、ボディソープあり。地元の方ばかりで、皆さん「やあ、久しぶり」「お先です」と、ごあいさつしてます。市外の方400円（大人）、市内の方300円（同）。  \n貴重品のロッカーは、100円（靴箱の横、返金あり）と、10円（脱衣所）。  \n[Image 20]【8†肥後biker†www.google.com】  \n 2024/1/2   \n市外在住者は400円、シャンプー、ボディーソープ備え付け有り。ドライヤー有ります、ロッカーは10円必要で小さめです。  \n浴場は大きめの内湯と小さめの低温湯があります。露天に寝そべり湯（使用不可中）打たせ湯（冷水）、湯船はありません、サウナも外にあります。  \nお湯は若干、とろみのあるお湯で無臭、湯温は丁度いい熱さ（恐らく41℃ほど）でした。  \n休憩所は広く、長めのソファやテーブル席が有り、休憩室もありました。  \n[Image 21]【9†17 K,s†www.google.com】  \n 2023/6/5   \n家族で初入浴。宇土市外は、100円たかいがそれでも400円は、安い。大人2名、子供1名なのだが、家族割との事で、100円引きとの事で、チケットを大人1枚、小人2枚で購入して下さいと言われました。結果100円引きだが、ちょっと複雑（笑）。お風呂は、露天風呂は、無いが、サウナや、水風呂もあり、満足。またサウナに小さいがテレビもあるので、長居してしまう。地元の方が多いので、サウナは、一部の方が場所取りや、大声で話しているのでマナーは、悪い。水風呂は、推定20度位なので、爽快感が足りない。この時季は、名前の通り、アジサイがいっぱい咲いていて、子供も喜んでました。             "これからの「地域の図書館」は、現代の日本社会と地方自治体の状況を踏まえて、以下のような方向性を目指すべきだと考えられます：\n\n### 1. **コミュニティセンターとしての機能強化**\n地域の図書館は、単なる本の貸出場所を超えて、地域住民の交流の場としての役割を強化する必要があります。多くの地方自治体では人口減少や高齢化が進んでおり、住民の孤立感を軽減するために、図書館がコミュニティセンターとしての機能を果たすことが重要です。イベントやワークショップ、地域の歴史や文化に関する展示など、住民が集まり交流する機会を提供する場として活用されることが期待されます。\n\n### 2. **デジタルサービスの充実**\nデジタル化が進む現代において、地域の図書館もデジタルサービスを充実させる必要があります。電子書籍の貸出サービスやオンラインでの情報検索、リモートアクセスが可能なデータベースの提供などが求められます。また、インターネットアクセスやデジタルリテラシー教育の場としても機能することが期待されます【17†source】【20†source】。\n\n### 3. **多世代共生の場としての役割**\n高齢者から子どもまで、多世代が利用できる場所としての図書館の役割を強化することが重要です。高齢者向けの読書会や子ども向けの読み聞かせイベント、学習支援プログラムなど、多様な世代に対応するサービスを提供することが求められます。これにより、世代間交流が促進され、地域全体の結束が強まることが期待されます【19†source】【42†source】。\n\n### 4. **地域資源の保存と活用**\n地域の歴史や文化を保存し、次世代に伝える役割も図書館に求められます。地域の資料や写真、歴史的な文献などを収集・保存し、展示やデジタルアーカイブとして公開することで、地域のアイデンティティを強化することができます。また、地域の伝統行事や文化活動の情報発信拠点としても機能することが重要です【18†source】。\n\n### 5. **持続可能な運営モデルの確立**\n地方自治体の財政状況が厳しい中で、図書館の持続可能な運営モデルを確立することが求められます。地域住民や企業との協力による資金調達やボランティアの活用など、柔軟な運営体制を構築することが必要です。また、省エネ設備の導入やエコイベントの開催など、環境に配慮した取り組みも重要です【19†source】【41†source】。\n\nこれらの方向性を実現するために、地域の図書館は地域住民や自治体、企業などと協力しながら、地域のニーズに応じたサービスを提供し、地域社会の中心としての役割を果たしていくことが求められます。"
          ]
        },

                "title": "Outsourcing in 2024: Trends and Predictions",
                "url": "https://www.globalresponse.com/blog/future-outsourcing-trends-predictions-2024/",
                "text": "\nNearshore outsourcing is when businesses work with providers located geographically close to them instead of halfway around the world. This has placed a greater emphasis on outsourcing partners in Central and Latin America. Eastern European countries like Poland, Ukraine, and Romania are also gaining popularity.\n\nWhy? With these countries, there is less of a language barrier, less of a time difference, and less of a culture gap. It makes outsourcing that much more seamless.\n\n## Changing Business Priorities and Outsourcing \n\nWith the advancement of technology, businesses are changing what and how they outsource. Previously, a company might work with multiple service providers (known as multi-vendor outsourcing). This allowed companies to diversify their talent pool and cover a range of tasks.\n\nBut today, businesses can use AI and RPA to take care of the menial tasks they once outsourced. Instead, businesses are looking for:\n\n  * Strong partnerships \n  * High-quality service \n  * Help with high-level tasks \n\nBusinesses are more interested in working closely with a particular team to build trusted, long-term relationships. While some still outsource menial tasks, many companies are looking for knowledge process outsourcing (KPO). KPO tasks include research and development, data analysis, decision support, and process improvement.\n\n## Impact of Remote Work on Outsourcing Trends \n\nOutsourcing has always relied on teams that were not physically present. For years, that felt very foreign to the average 9-5 business person. Not anymore: The rise of remote work has normalized this business model.\n\nAs a result, traditional outsourcing models are evolving to accommodate remote work trends. How?\n\nMore and more businesses are using hybrid outsourcing models combining on-site and remote resources. This allows for greater flexibility and a balance between in-house and external expertise.\n\nRemote work has expanded the talent pool and increased diversity among teams. More than ever, companies can tap into the global talent pool. They have access to specialized skills and expertise, regardless of physical location. Teams comprise members from different regions, cultures, and backgrounds, leading to a more diverse and inclusive work environment.\n\nRemote work has led to a shift toward client-centric outsourcing models. Clients are demanding more transparency, real-time communication, and collaborative decision-making. This fosters a partnership approach rather than a traditional client-vendor relationship.\n\nThe remote work paradigm has prompted a greater focus on employee well-being and mental health. Companies are increasingly supporting the well-being of their remote and outsourced teams to ensure sustained productivity and job satisfaction.\n\n## Outsourcing and Data Security: Future Challenges \n\nWe can’t discuss outsourcing trends without considering the serious threat of cyberattacks and data breaches. In 2023 alone, the average data breach cost【51† $4.45 million for businesses worldwide†www.ibm.com】.\n\nBusinesses’ reliance on digital platforms will only continue to grow. Both businesses and outsourcing partners must invest in cybersecurity to protect data and maintain trust.\n\n10 Ways to Protect Your Business from Cyberattacks in 2024 \n\n  1. Conduct thorough due diligence before selecting an outsourcing partner. Assess their security policies, practices, and compliance with relevant regulations. Evaluate the partner’s track record in data security and their experience in handling sensitive information.\n  2. Clearly define security requirements in outsourcing contracts. Establish specific clauses related to data protection, confidentiality, and compliance. Contracts should outline the security standards expected from the outsourcing partner and mechanisms for addressing breaches.\n  3. Implement robust encryption protocols for data both in transit and at rest. Encrypting sensitive information adds an extra layer of protection. This makes it more challenging for unauthorized entities to access or misuse the data.\n  4. Implement robust access controls and authentication mechanisms. Ensure only authorized personnel can access sensitive data, and employ multi-factor authentication to enhance security.\n  5. Conduct regular security audits and assessments of the outsourcing partner’s systems. This includes penetration testing, vulnerability assessments, and security reviews to identify and address potential weaknesses in the infrastructure.\n  6. Provide comprehensive security training to both internal staff and the employees of the outsourcing partner. Ensure that all personnel know security best practices, the importance of data protection, and the potential consequences of security breaches.\n  7. Implement continuous monitoring of network activities and establish an effective incident response plan. Real-time monitoring helps detect unusual activities or potential breaches. This allows you to respond promptly and mitigate any security threats.\n  8. Understand and comply with data protection laws and regulations applicable to the outsourcing arrangement. Clearly define data residency requirements and ensure the outsourcing partner adheres to relevant legal standards.\n  9. Foster open communication with the outsourcing partner regarding security concerns and practices. Establish a collaborative approach to address security challenges and share best practices for maintaining a secure environment.\n  10. Consider cybersecurity insurance to mitigate financial risks associated with potential data breaches. Insurance coverage offsets the costs of investigating and remediating a breach. It can also help cover legal and regulatory consequences.\n\n## Regulatory Changes and Their Impact on Outsourcing \n\nStaying abreast of regulatory changes is critical when outsourcing to service providers. Your company is working across international borders, and with that comes a host of rules and regulations.\n\nHere are a handful of areas to keep an eye on to ensure you comply with regulatory changes:\n\n  1. Data Protection and Privacy Laws: As previously discussed, this is a big issue for companies that outsource today. Data protection regulations, such as the General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR), place strict requirements on how personal data is handled, processed, and transferred. Be mindful of changes in these laws, as they will impact your outsourcing contracts and business practices.\n  2. Labor Laws and Employment Regulations: Stay up-to-date on labor laws and employment regulations. Regularly check laws regarding the treatment of workers, contractual obligations, and compliance with labor standards. It is important to provide fair wages and good working conditions for both in-house and outsourced employees.\n  3. T",
